| node:navigation.route#1                | real_defect (0.84)       | missing_attribute (0.82)         | 2.5 | 1.2 | 0.29  | 0.08 | navigation.route has no path or name attribute, so the defining URL pattern and route parameters cannot be recorded.                                                                                                                                    | Add path (URL pattern), name and route parameters attributes to navigation.route.                                                                                               |
| node:navigation.route#2                | real_defect (0.71)       | enum_gap (0.82)                  | 2.2 | 1.9 | 0.21  | 0.08 | The navigation.route type enum mixes three independent dimensions: access (public/protected), behaviour (redirect/alias) and loading (lazy), so a protected lazy route cannot be expressed.                                                             | Split the type enum into separate attributes for access, behaviour and loading.                                                                                                 |
| rel:navigation.navigationgraph#1       | real_defect (0.82)       | relationship_redundant (0.72)    | 1.9 | 1.8 | 0.06  | 0.04 | navigation.navigationgraph has both aggregates and composes relationships to navigation.route, which gives contradictory lifecycle semantics for the same pair.                                                                                         | Keep only the composes relationship from navigationgraph to route and remove the aggregates relationship.                                                                       |
| node:navigation.routemeta#1            | real_defect (0.62)       | attribute_typing (0.87)          | 1.9 | 1.9 | 0.13  | 0.04 | The attributes roles, permissions, layout, keepAlive and transition of navigation.routemeta are marked required, but real meta objects set only a few fields, so they should be optional.                                                               | Make roles, permissions, layout, keepAlive and transition optional (not required).                                                                                              |
| node:navigation.guardaction#1          | real_defect (0.76)       | attribute_typing (0.85)          | 1.8 | 1.9 | 0.07  | 0.03 | The attributes 'target' and 'message' on navigation.guardaction are marked required although they apply only to some actions, so they should be conditionally required.                                                                                 | Make target required only for redirect actions and message required only for notify and prompt actions.                                                                         |
| node:navigation.navigationtransition#4 | real_defect (0.75)       | attribute_typing (0.39)          | 1.8 | 1.9 | 0.34  | 0.13 | The string attributes 'from' and 'to' on NavigationTransition duplicate the edges to Route and are untyped references.                                                                                                                                  | Remove the from/to string attributes and rely on relationships to Route (or make them typed Route references).                                                                  |
| node:navigation.navigationguard#1      | real_defect (0.43)       | enum_gap (0.79)                  | 1.8 | 1.0 | 0.36  | 0.16 | The enum attribute 'type' of navigation.navigationguard lacks a value for leave/deactivate guards such as unsaved-changes prompts.                                                                                                                      | Add an enum value (e.g. 'deactivate' or 'unsaved-changes') to navigationguard.type.                                                                                             |
| rel:navigation.navigationflow#1        | real_defect (0.85)       | relationship_redundant (0.79) ×3 | 1.8 | 1.8 | 0.06  | 0.08 | NavigationFlow has both aggregates and composes relationships to FlowStep, which conflict; since a FlowStep exists only within its flow, composes is correct.                                                                                           | Drop the aggregates relationship from navigation.navigationflow to navigation.flowstep and keep composes.                                                                       |
| node:navigation.contextvariable#4      | real_defect (0.58)       | missing_attribute (0.69)         | 1.8 | 1.2 | 0.61  | 0.06 | navigation.contextvariable declares no name attribute, although it is meant to be a named variable.                                                                                                                                                     | Add a name attribute to navigation.contextvariable.                                                                                                                             |
| rel:navigation.navigationgraph#2       | real_defect (0.78)       | relationship_redundant (0.72)    | 1.7 | 1.9 | 0.05  | 0.05 | navigation.navigationgraph has both aggregates and composes relationships to navigation.navigationtransition, which gives contradictory lifecycle semantics for the same pair.                                                                          | Keep only the composes relationship from navigationgraph to navigationtransition and remove the aggregates relationship.                                                        |
| node:navigation.flowstep#4             | real_defect (0.51)       | missing_attribute (0.73)         | 1.6 | 1.0 | 0.03  | 0.03 | The waitBehavior enum value 'timeout' on navigation.flowstep has no accompanying timeout-duration attribute to specify the timeout.                                                                                                                     | Add a timeout duration attribute to flowstep for use with waitBehavior 'timeout'.                                                                                               |
| node:navigation.breadcrumbconfig#1     | real_defect (0.66)       | missing_attribute (0.58)         | 1.6 | 1.0 | 0.13  | 0.04 | The definition of navigation.breadcrumbconfig promises separator styles, truncation behavior and path generation rules, but no attributes exist to capture them.                                                                                        | Add separator, maxItems and truncationStrategy attributes to navigation.breadcrumbconfig.                                                                                       |
| rel:navigation.routemeta#1             | real_defect (0.77)       | relationship_redundant (0.70)    | 1.5 | 1.7 | 0.08  | 0.09 | The 'serves' relationship from navigation.routemeta to navigation.route is weak because route metadata is part of the route, so composition (Route composes RouteMeta) fits better; this duplicates the existing route-composes-routemeta relationship. | Remove the routemeta serves route relationship and rely on navigation.route composes navigation.routemeta.                                                                      |
| node:navigation.flowstep#1             | real_defect (0.40)       | relationship_redundant (0.54)    | 1.5 | 1.9 | 0.12  | 0.13 | The 'route' string attribute on navigation.flowstep duplicates the navigates-to and realizes relationships from flowstep to navigation.route, so only one representation should be kept.                                                                | Keep only one of the route attribute or the flowstep-to-route relationship and remove the duplicate.                                                                            |
| rel:navigation.route#4                 | real_defect (0.62)       | definition_conflict (0.36)       | 1.5 | 1.9 | 0.10  | 0.05 | Direct relationships from the client route navigation.route to data-store elements (Collection, View, AccessPattern) bypass the API layer and look dubious.                                                                                             | Remove or reconsider direct route-to-data-store relationships so access goes through the API layer.                                                                             |
| node:navigation.navigationflow#2       | real_defect (0.36)       | missing_attribute (0.63) ×2      | 1.5 | 1.1 | 0.57  | 0.08 | navigation.navigationflow lacks an explicit name attribute.                                                                                                                                                                                             | Add a name attribute to navigation.navigationflow.                                                                                                                              |
| rel:navigation.navigationtransition#1  | real_defect (0.51)       | relationship_redundant (0.84)    | 1.5 | 1.8 | 0.06  | 0.07 | The relationships navigation.navigationtransition flows-to navigation.route and navigation.navigationtransition navigates-to navigation.route are redundant because both express the transition's destination route.                                    | Keep navigates-to (the UX-specific predicate) and remove flows-to from navigationtransition to route.                                                                           |
| rel:navigation.routemeta#2             | real_defect (0.71)       | relationship_direction (0.58)    | 1.5 | 1.3 | 0.60  | 0.56 | The 'uses' relationship from navigation.routemeta to navigation.navigationtransition conflates the visual animation named in the meta 'transition' field with the NavigationTransition element, which is a route-change edge.                           | Remove or rework the uses relationship to navigation.navigationtransition so it no longer equates the animation name with the route-change edge element.                        |
| rel:navigation.route#1                 | real_defect (0.53)       | relationship_redundant (0.84)    | 1.4 | 1.8 | 0.08  | 0.11 | The relationships navigation.route associated-with navigation.routemeta and navigation.route composes navigation.routemeta express the same meaning and are redundant.                                                                                  | Remove associated-with and keep composes between navigation.route and navigation.routemeta.                                                                                     |
| rel:navigation.breadcrumbconfig#1      | real_defect (0.56)       | relationship_direction (0.70)    | 1.4 | 1.3 | 0.12  | 0.22 | The relationship navigation.breadcrumbconfig provides navigation.routemeta appears inverted, because RouteMeta normally supplies the breadcrumb label that BreadcrumbConfig consumes.                                                                   | Reverse the relationship so RouteMeta provides to BreadcrumbConfig, or replace it with a consumes/reads-from relationship from BreadcrumbConfig to RouteMeta.                   |
| rel:navigation.flowstep#3              | real_defect (0.41)       | relationship_direction (0.50)    | 1.4 | 1.8 | 0.07  | 0.06 | The accesses relationships from navigation.flowstep to data-store.collection, data-model.objectschema and api.operation are indirect because a client-side step does not access them directly.                                                          | Remove these direct accesses relationships from FlowStep.                                                                                                                       |
| rel:navigation.contextvariable#1       | real_defect (0.55)       | relationship_direction (0.84)    | 1.4 | 1.5 | 0.10  | 0.07 | The relationship navigation.contextvariable consumes navigation.route gives agent-like behavior to passive runtime data and its direction is wrong; the route should provide or populate the variable.                                                  | Invert it so navigation.route provides or populates navigation.contextvariable, or remove the consumes relationship from contextvariable to route.                              |
| rel:navigation.routemeta#3             | real_defect (0.81)       | relationship_direction (0.83)    | 1.4 | 1.2 | 0.25  | 0.34 | The 'governs' relationship from navigation.routemeta to navigation.navigationguard overstates the role of route metadata, which only supplies hints while the guard does the governing.                                                                 | Change the predicate from 'governs' to 'references'.                                                                                                                            |
| rel:navigation.guardcondition#1        | real_defect (0.59)       | relationship_redundant (0.89) ×3 | 1.4 | 1.7 | 0.11  | 0.08 | The consumes and uses relationships from navigation.guardcondition to navigation.contextvariable express the same meaning (the condition reads flow state), so they are redundant.                                                                      | Keep only one of the two relationships, preferably consumes or references, and remove the other.                                                                                |
| node:navigation.contextvariable#7      | real_defect (0.54)       | relationship_direction (0.82) ×2 | 1.3 | 1.5 | 0.12  | 0.05 | A 'triggers' relationship from the passive navigation.contextvariable to navigation.navigationtransition is questionable because passive state does not trigger transitions.                                                                            | Remove the triggers edge or replace it with a more fitting predicate or direction.                                                                                              |
| node:navigation.guardcondition#1       | real_defect (0.62)       | attribute_typing (0.84)          | 1.3 | 1.6 | 0.24  | 0.03 | The timeout attribute of navigation.guardcondition is required even when async is false, which is meaningless for synchronous predicates.                                                                                                               | Make timeout optional or conditionally required only when async is true.                                                                                                        |
| node:navigation.guardaction#3          | real_defect (0.43)       | definition_conflict (0.52)       | 1.3 | 0.4 | 0.53  | 0.20 | The 'prompt' action typically arises from leave guards such as canDeactivate rather than access denial, so the definition's 'access denied' framing of navigation.guardaction is too narrow.                                                            | Broaden the definition to cover leave guards and confirmation prompts, not only access denial.                                                                                  |
| rel:navigation.breadcrumbconfig#2      | real_defect (0.47)       | relationship_redundant (0.77)    | 1.3 | 1.3 | 0.16  | 0.06 | The relationship navigation.breadcrumbconfig associated-with navigation.navigationgraph is weak, since the graph's scope implies composition or aggregation.                                                                                            | Replace the weak associated-with relationship with a composition or aggregation relationship (or drop it in favor of the existing navigationgraph aggregates breadcrumbconfig). |

| node:navigation.flowstep#2             | judgment_call (0.45)     | relationship_direction (0.70)    | 1.1 | 1.3 | 0.10  | 0.06 | The 'realizes' relationship from navigation.flowstep to navigation.route is semantically odd.                                                                                                                                                           | Remove or replace the realizes predicate with a more fitting one (e.g. keep navigates-to).                                                                                      |
| rel:navigation.flowstep#5              | judgment_call (0.56)     | relationship_direction (0.57)    | 1.1 | 1.6 | 0.04  | 0.05 | The relationship navigation.flowstep realizes business.businessfunction is weak because a flow step is ordered while business functions are unordered.                                                                                                  | Remove the realizes relationship from FlowStep to BusinessFunction.                                                                                                             |
| rel:navigation.navigationflow#5        | judgment_call (0.34)     | relationship_direction (0.57)    | 1.1 | 1.7 | 0.05  | 0.04 | The uses relationship from NavigationFlow to technology.systemsoftware is weakly meaningful for a client-side flow.                                                                                                                                     | Remove the uses relationship to technology.systemsoftware.                                                                                                                      |
| rel:navigation.navigationflow#2        | judgment_call (0.64)     | relationship_direction (0.71)    | 1.1 | 1.5 | 0.04  | 0.05 | The realizes relationship from NavigationFlow to motivation.goal is questionable, since supports fits better than realizing a goal.                                                                                                                     | Replace realizes with supports for the NavigationFlow to Goal relationship.                                                                                                     |
| node:navigation.routemeta#3            | judgment_call (0.36)     | attribute_naming (0.83)          | 1.0 | 1.4 | 0.30  | 0.52 | The 'transition' attribute of navigation.routemeta collides with the NavigationTransition element type and the uses→navigationtransition edge.                                                                                                          | Rename the attribute 'transition' to 'animation'.                                                                                                                               |
| rel:navigation.navigationguard#3       | judgment_call (0.47)     | relationship_direction (0.74)    | 1.0 | 1.3 | 0.18  | 0.05 | The implements relationship from navigation.navigationguard to security.securitypolicy overstates what a client-side guard does; enforces fits better, and real enforcement belongs on the server.                                                      | Replace implements with enforces.                                                                                                                                               |
| rel:navigation.contextvariable#4       | judgment_call (0.46)     | relationship_redundant (0.73)    | 1.0 | 0.7 | 0.09  | 0.07 | The relationships navigation.contextvariable references application.dataobject and navigation.contextvariable references data-model.jsonschema both describe the value type of the variable and are redundant.                                          | none stated                                                                                                                                                                     |
| node:navigation.contextvariable#1      | judgment_call (0.45)     | enum_gap (0.55)                  | 1.0 | 0.8 | 0.22  | 0.06 | The scope enum value 'session' of navigation.contextvariable overlaps with the persistedIn value 'sessionStorage', so the two attributes mix lifetime and storage axes.                                                                                 | none stated                                                                                                                                                                     |
| rel:navigation.contextvariable#3       | judgment_call (0.45)     | relationship_redundant (0.65)    | 0.9 | 0.4 | 0.13  | 0.11 | The relationship navigation.contextvariable references data-model.jsonschema overlaps with navigation.contextvariable maps-to data-model.schemaproperty when the variable's schema is a property subschema.                                             | none stated                                                                                                                                                                     |
| rel:navigation.navigationgraph#3       | judgment_call (0.48)     | relationship_direction (0.58)    | 0.9 | 1.0 | 0.11  | 0.04 | The uses relationship from navigation.navigationgraph to technology.systemsoftware is weak, because a front-end route table depends mainly on a router library or the browser rather than on host system software.                                      | none stated                                                                                                                                                                     |
| node:navigation.navigationgraph#4      | judgment_call (0.48)     | missing_attribute (0.67) ×2      | 0.9 | 1.0 | 0.11  | 0.04 | NavigationGraph lacks attributes capturing the router framework or history mode.                                                                                                                                                                        | Consider adding 'routerFramework' or 'historyMode' attributes to NavigationGraph.                                                                                               |
| node:navigation.flowstep#5             | judgment_call (0.52)     | attribute_naming (0.89)          | 0.9 | 1.6 | 0.17  | 0.08 | The 'required' attribute name on navigation.flowstep collides with ContextVariable.required, and 'skippable' or 'optional' would be clearer.                                                                                                            | Rename 'required' to 'skippable' or 'optional'.                                                                                                                                 |
| rel:navigation.navigationflow#4        | judgment_call (0.56)     | relationship_direction (0.73)    | 0.9 | 1.4 | 0.09  | 0.08 | The flows-to predicate from NavigationFlow to NavigationTransition is odd, since a flow traverses or references transitions.                                                                                                                            | Change the predicate from flows-to to traverses or references.                                                                                                                  |
| rel:navigation.flowstep#1              | works_as_designed (0.39) | relationship_redundant (0.80)    | 1.5 | 1.8 | 0.08  | 0.13 | The relationships navigation.flowstep navigates-to navigation.route and navigation.flowstep realizes navigation.route both express the step's binding to one route, so they are redundant.                                                              | Keep only one of navigates-to or realizes between FlowStep and Route and remove the other.                                                                                      |
| node:navigation.navigationgraph#3      | works_as_designed (0.32) | missing_attribute (0.29) ×2      | 1.4 | 0.8 | 0.75  | 0.13 | No cardinality rule is defined for NavigationGraph in applications with micro-frontends or multiple router instances.                                                                                                                                   | Define a cardinality rule for NavigationGraph per application for micro-frontends or multiple router instances.                                                                 |
| node:navigation.route#4                | works_as_designed (0.51) | relationship_redundant (0.72) ×2 | 1.4 | 2.0 | 0.14  | 0.23 | The flows-to, triggers and navigates-to relationships from navigation.route look redundant with each other.                                                                                                                                             | Consolidate flows-to, triggers and navigates-to into a single relationship.                                                                                                     |
| node:navigation.navigationtransition#2 | works_as_designed (0.38) | relationship_redundant (0.80)    | 1.4 | 1.7 | 0.33  | 0.16 | NavigationTransition has redundant relationships: flows-to and navigates-to both target Route.                                                                                                                                                          | Remove one of flows-to or navigates-to, keeping a single relationship from NavigationTransition to Route.                                                                       |
| node:navigation.navigationguard#3      | works_as_designed (0.31) | missing_attribute (0.67) ×2      | 1.3 | 1.0 | 0.31  | 0.10 | navigation.navigationguard has no attribute capturing guard scope (global versus per-route).                                                                                                                                                            | Add a scope attribute (e.g. 'scope' with values global/per-route) to navigationguard.                                                                                           |
| rel:navigation.guardaction#2           | works_as_designed (0.39) | relationship_missing (0.61)      | 1.3 | 1.0 | 0.05  | 0.07 | The catalog lacks a Guard-level 'evaluates' relationship from NavigationGuard to GuardCondition and a composes relationship from NavigationGuard to GuardAction, which would express the evaluate-then-invoke flow more accurately.                     | Add navigationguard evaluates guardcondition and navigationguard composes guardaction relationships.                                                                            |
| node:navigation.route#3                | works_as_designed (0.41) | relationship_redundant (0.79) ×2 | 1.2 | 1.5 | 0.09  | 0.11 | The aggregates and composes relationships from navigation.route look redundant with each other.                                                                                                                                                         | Keep only one of aggregates or composes.                                                                                                                                        |
| rel:navigation.flowstep#2              | works_as_designed (0.34) | relationship_redundant (0.64)    | 1.2 | 1.8 | 0.09  | 0.10 | The relationship navigation.flowstep maps-to ux.view is a data-mapping predicate and duplicates the Route to View rendering relationship.                                                                                                               | Remove the maps-to relationship from FlowStep to View.                                                                                                                          |
| node:navigation.navigationflow#5       | works_as_designed (0.37) | attribute_typing (0.27)          | 1.1 | 0.8 | 0.23  | 0.14 | ContextVariable scope value 'flow' implies a flow identifier that is not modeled on NavigationFlow.                                                                                                                                                     | none stated                                                                                                                                                                     |
| node:navigation.guardcondition#2       | works_as_designed (0.35) | relationship_redundant (0.66)    | 1.1 | 1.7 | 0.40  | 0.08 | The 'constrains' relationship from navigation.guardcondition to navigation.route duplicates the role already played by the guard.                                                                                                                       | Remove the constrains relationship from guardcondition to route.                                                                                                                |
| rel:navigation.guardaction#3           | works_as_designed (0.52) | relationship_direction (0.53)    | 0.9 | 0.3 | 0.27  | 0.12 | The navigates-to relationship from navigation.guardaction to navigation.route applies only to redirect-type actions, not to block, notify or prompt (cancel, message, confirm) actions.                                                                 | none stated                                                                                                                                                                     |
| node:navigation.route#5                | works_as_designed (0.40) | type_overlap (0.84)              | 0.9 | 0.2 | 0.87  | 0.67 | The boundary between navigation.route and api.pathitem needs to be stated.                                                                                                                                                                              | State in the definition that navigation.route is a client-side route entry and api.pathitem is a server-side HTTP path.                                                         |
| node:navigation.navigationguard#5      | works_as_designed (0.54) | definition_conflict (0.59)       | 0.9 | 0.2 | 0.60  | 0.35 | RouteMeta states that guards live in the security layer, which contradicts navigationguard being placed in the navigation layer.                                                                                                                        | Reconcile the layer placement by fixing the RouteMeta wording or the navigationguard layer assignment.                                                                          |

## product

| id                          | validity (p)             | category (p)                     | sev | fix | addr. | clf. | claim                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | proposed change                                                                                                                                             |
| --------------------------- | ------------------------ | -------------------------------- | --- | --- | ----- | ---- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| node:product.milestone#1    | real_defect (0.73)       | missing_attribute (0.63) ×2      | 1.9 | 1.1 | 0.27  | 0.02 | The product.milestone type is defined as time-bound, but no attribute such as a target date supports this, so a targetDate (ISO 8601) attribute is missing.                                                                                   | Add a targetDate attribute in ISO 8601 format to product.milestone.                                                                                         |
| node:product.feature#1      | real_defect (0.47)       | relationship_redundant (0.77) ×2 | 1.4 | 0.7 | 0.50  | 0.06 | Both 'realizes' and 'uses' relationships from product.feature to product.capability exist without guidance on when to use each, so they are redundant or ambiguous.                                                                           | Define when to use 'realizes' versus 'uses' between product.feature and product.capability, or drop the 'uses' relationship.                                |
| rel:product.persona#1       | real_defect (0.61)       | relationship_direction (0.83)    | 1.4 | 1.5 | 0.13  | 0.06 | The relationship 'product.persona serves business.businessrole' is semantically wrong because a persona does not provide service to a role; it describes the people who fill the role.                                                        | Replace the 'serves' predicate with 'associated-with' or 'references'.                                                                                      |
| node:product.workflowstep#1 | real_defect (0.53)       | definition_conflict (0.52)       | 1.3 | 0.5 | 0.10  | 0.04 | The product.workflowstep sequence attribute and the flows-to relationships between workflow steps can contradict each other, and the spec does not define which one is authoritative for ordering.                                            | Define which of sequence or flows-to is authoritative for step order (or how they must be kept consistent).                                                 |
| node:product.workflowstep#5 | real_defect (0.48)       | relationship_redundant (0.63)    | 1.3 | 1.5 | 0.09  | 0.03 | The flows-to relationship from product.workflowstep is listed twice in the catalog.                                                                                                                                                           | Remove the duplicate flows-to relationship entry.                                                                                                           |
| node:product.persona#1      | real_defect (0.68)       | enum_gap (0.73)                  | 1.3 | 1.5 | 0.03  | 0.04 | The product.persona.category enum mixes importance values (primary, secondary) with Cooper's persona kinds (served, negative), so its description 'by importance' is misleading.                                                              | Split the enum into separate axes (importance vs. Cooper kind) or correct the description so it no longer says 'by importance'.                             |
| rel:product.persona#2       | real_defect (0.42)       | relationship_direction (0.84)    | 1.2 | 1.3 | 0.07  | 0.04 | The relationship 'product.persona realizes motivation.stakeholder' is questionable because a fictional archetype does not implement a stakeholder.                                                                                            | Replace 'realizes' with 'associated-with' or introduce a dedicated 'represents' predicate.                                                                  |
| node:product.persona#3      | real_defect (0.33)       | type_overlap (0.83)              | 1.2 | 0.4 | 0.83  | 0.45 | The boundary between product.persona and business.businessrole is fuzzy, since authorization roles such as admin or editor are roles rather than personas.                                                                                    | Define a boundary rule stating that auth/access roles belong to business.businessrole and personas are user archetypes.                                     |
| node:product.capability#2   | real_defect (0.37)       | relationship_redundant (0.69)    | 1.2 | 0.5 | 0.49  | 0.11 | Feature has both 'realizes' and 'uses' relationships toward product.capability without a stated distinction between them.                                                                                                                     | Document the semantic distinction between 'realizes' and 'uses' from feature to capability, or remove one.                                                  |
| node:product.milestone#4    | real_defect (0.57)       | relationship_redundant (0.83)    | 1.2 | 1.6 | 0.19  | 0.03 | The precedes relationship on product.milestone is listed twice, making it redundant.                                                                                                                                                          | Remove the duplicate precedes relationship entry for product.milestone.                                                                                     |
| node:product.feature#2      | real_defect (0.44)       | enum_gap (0.75)                  | 1.1 | 1.7 | 0.03  | 0.03 | The 'informational' value of the product.feature priority enum reads like a severity level and does not fit a deliverable.                                                                                                                    | Remove or replace the 'informational' value in the priority enum.                                                                                           |
| node:product.userworkflow#2 | real_defect (0.43)       | attribute_typing (0.29)          | 1.1 | 0.2 | 0.06  | 0.02 | The complexity enum on product.userworkflow (simple/moderate/complex) has no decision criteria for choosing a value.                                                                                                                          | Add guidance defining complexity levels by step count or branching.                                                                                         |
| node:product.persona#2      | judgment_call (0.42)     | missing_attribute (0.61)         | 1.5 | 1.0 | 0.11  | 0.03 | The product.persona definition cites goals and pain points, but no attributes on product.persona capture goals or pain points.                                                                                                                | Add attributes such as goals and painPoints to product.persona.                                                                                             |
| node:product.workflowstep#4 | judgment_call (0.33)     | relationship_missing (0.67)      | 1.5 | 1.1 | 0.06  | 0.04 | product.workflowstep has no way to name which Persona performs a step when a workflow involves several actors, other than the general 'serves' link.                                                                                          | Add an explicit performer relationship or attribute linking a step to the Persona who performs it.                                                          |
| rel:product.userworkflow#2  | judgment_call (0.40)     | relationship_direction (0.89)    | 1.5 | 1.7 | 0.06  | 0.04 | The relationship product.userworkflow aggregates business.businessprocess looks inverted; a user journey more naturally supports or realizes part of a business process.                                                                      | Replace aggregates with supports or realizes, with the workflow supporting or realizing the business process.                                               |
| node:product.userworkflow#3 | judgment_call (0.39)     | missing_attribute (0.68)         | 1.3 | 1.0 | 0.26  | 0.04 | product.userworkflow has no attribute capturing the workflow's goal or its trigger.                                                                                                                                                           | Add 'goal' and 'trigger' attributes to product.userworkflow.                                                                                                |
| node:product.capability#1   | judgment_call (0.36)     | relationship_direction (0.80)    | 1.3 | 1.4 | 0.25  | 0.04 | The outgoing 'realizes' relationship from product.capability to business.businessfunction inverts ArchiMate, where behaviour realizes capability.                                                                                             | Replace 'realizes' with 'serves' or 'associated-with' for product.capability to business.businessfunction.                                                  |
| rel:product.userworkflow#1  | judgment_call (0.59)     | relationship_direction (0.74) ×2 | 1.2 | 1.4 | 0.14  | 0.06 | The relationship product.userworkflow composes product.feature is semantically wrong because features exist independently of any workflow and are shared across journeys.                                                                     | Replace composes with aggregates or uses for the userworkflow-to-feature relationship.                                                                      |
| node:product.capability#3   | judgment_call (0.60)     | missing_attribute (0.67)         | 1.2 | 1.0 | 0.04  | 0.04 | product.capability lacks a granularity or level attribute (e.g. L1/L2) to support capability hierarchies built via 'aggregates'.                                                                                                              | Add a granularity/level attribute (e.g. enum L1/L2/L3) to product.capability.                                                                               |
| node:product.persona#4      | judgment_call (0.42)     | relationship_direction (0.87)    | 1.2 | 1.3 | 0.11  | 0.04 | The relationship 'product.persona serves business.businessrole' appears to point the wrong way or use an ill-fitting predicate.                                                                                                               | Replace 'serves' with 'aggregates' or 'associated with', or reverse the direction.                                                                          |
| rel:product.workflowstep#1  | judgment_call (0.60)     | relationship_redundant (0.81)    | 1.2 | 1.2 | 0.08  | 0.06 | The relationships product.workflowstep uses product.capability and product.workflowstep uses product.feature overlap, because features realise capabilities and the step-to-capability link can be derived via step-to-feature-to-capability. | Keep the workflowstep-uses-capability relationship only for steps whose feature is not yet defined, treating it otherwise as derived from the feature link. |
| node:product.milestone#2    | judgment_call (0.62)     | missing_attribute (0.72) ×2      | 1.2 | 1.0 | 0.18  | 0.03 | The product.milestone type may also need an optional startDate or version label attribute to capture its time period or release identity.                                                                                                     | Optionally add startDate and a version label attribute to product.milestone.                                                                                |
| rel:product.feature#2       | judgment_call (0.46)     | relationship_redundant (0.65)    | 1.2 | 0.8 | 0.07  | 0.08 | The product.feature uses relationship to product.capability risks being confused with the realizes relationship to the same capability, so a feature should not both realize and use the same capability.                                     | Add an editor/validation rule that a feature must not both realize and use the same capability.                                                             |
| node:product.capability#4   | judgment_call (0.43)     | enum_gap (0.49)                  | 1.1 | 1.3 | 0.12  | 0.26 | The status enum of product.capability duplicates that of product.feature, blurring lifecycle versus delivery status.                                                                                                                          | Differentiate capability status as lifecycle values distinct from feature's delivery status values.                                                         |
| rel:product.capability#1    | judgment_call (0.45)     | relationship_redundant (0.67)    | 1.1 | 1.3 | 0.05  | 0.05 | The relationship product.capability --[fulfills]--> motivation.requirement partially overlaps with the traceability predicate satisfies/fulfills-requirements, so a single convention for linking to requirements should be chosen.           | Choose one predicate convention (fulfills or satisfies) for capability-to-requirement traceability and remove or align the other.                           |
| node:product.userworkflow#1 | judgment_call (0.54)     | relationship_direction (0.67) ×2 | 1.1 | 1.3 | 0.56  | 0.08 | The 'composes' relationship from product.userworkflow to product.feature is questionable because a workflow exercises features through its steps rather than owning them.                                                                     | Replace 'composes' with 'uses' or route the connection through product.workflowstep.                                                                        |
| rel:product.feature#3       | judgment_call (0.54)     | relationship_redundant (0.78)    | 1.1 | 1.6 | 0.08  | 0.04 | The cross-layer product.feature realizes business.businessservice relationship may be redundant because application services usually realize business services, so the link is already implied via capability.                                | Consider removing the feature realizes businessservice relationship or relying on the path via capability.                                                  |
| node:product.workflowstep#3 | judgment_call (0.53)     | relationship_redundant (0.78)    | 1.0 | 1.3 | 0.10  | 0.07 | The 'uses capability' relationship from product.workflowstep duplicates what the Feature already delivers via the step's feature link.                                                                                                        | Remove or justify the workflowstep-to-capability 'uses' relationship since the Feature already covers it.                                                   |
| node:product.milestone#5    | judgment_call (0.54)     | relationship_missing (0.81)      | 1.0 | 1.1 | 0.05  | 0.02 | A completed product.milestone may need to link to the release tag that realized it, and no such relationship exists.                                                                                                                          | Consider adding a relationship from a completed milestone to the release tag that realized it.                                                              |
| node:product.feature#4      | judgment_call (0.31)     | relationship_redundant (0.52) ×2 | 1.0 | 0.8 | 0.03  | 0.03 | The depends-on self-relationship of product.feature appears in both the outgoing and incoming relationship lists, duplicating it.                                                                                                             | List the depends-on self-relationship only once, not in both outgoing and incoming lists.                                                                   |
| rel:product.capability#2    | judgment_call (0.49)     | relationship_redundant (0.61)    | 1.0 | 0.3 | 0.08  | 0.07 | The product.capability --[aggregates]--> product.capability relationship could be confused with composes; aggregation is only appropriate if sub-capabilities can be shared or regrouped.                                                     | Clarify that aggregates applies only when sub-capabilities can be shared or regrouped, and use composes otherwise.                                          |
| rel:product.userworkflow#3  | judgment_call (0.65)     | relationship_redundant (0.78)    | 1.0 | 1.6 | 0.10  | 0.04 | The relationship product.userworkflow uses product.capability is largely derivable via workflow steps to features to capabilities, so it is partly redundant.                                                                                 | Consider removing the direct uses relationship to capability since it is derivable through steps and features.                                              |
| rel:product.workflowstep#2  | judgment_call (0.53)     | relationship_direction (0.87)    | 0.9 | 1.5 | 0.02  | 0.04 | The relationship product.workflowstep serves product.persona uses a loose predicate, since the persona performs the step; 'performed-by' would read more naturally.                                                                           | Replace 'serves' with a 'performed-by' predicate for the workflowstep-to-persona relationship.                                                              |
| rel:product.feature#1       | judgment_call (0.45)     | relationship_redundant (0.66)    | 0.9 | 0.4 | 0.12  | 0.04 | The product.feature delivers-value relationship to motivation.value overlaps with delivers/supports relationships from the motivation layer, which the note considers acceptable.                                                             | none stated                                                                                                                                                 |
| node:product.feature#3      | judgment_call (0.38)     | attribute_naming (0.27)          | 0.8 | 0.1 | 0.05  | 0.02 | The product.feature size enum (xs/s/m/l/xl) lacks a stated scale, so it is unclear whether it means story points or effort.                                                                                                                   | State the scale the size enum represents (e.g. story points or effort) in the attribute description.                                                        |
| node:product.milestone#3    | judgment_call (0.53)     | attribute_naming (0.23)          | 0.3 | 0.1 | 0.17  | 0.05 | The product.milestone.status enum (proposed, planned, active, completed, cancelled) differs from the capability/feature lifecycle, which is acceptable but should be documented.                                                              | Document the reason the milestone status enum differs from the capability/feature lifecycle.                                                                |
| node:product.workflowstep#2 | works_as_designed (0.39) | missing_attribute (0.48)         | 1.3 | 1.4 | 0.24  | 0.06 | product.workflowstep cannot express branching or alternative paths in a workflow.                                                                                                                                                             | none stated                                                                                                                                                 |

## security

| id                                        | validity (p)             | category (p)                     | sev | fix | addr. | clf. | claim                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | proposed change                                                                                                                                                               |
| ----------------------------------------- | ------------------------ | -------------------------------- | --- | --- | ----- | ---- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| node:security.role#1                      | real_defect (0.74)       | attribute_typing (0.68) ×2       | 2.5 | 1.8 | 0.87  | 0.03 | The attribute security.role.inheritsFrom is required, but root roles have no parent role, so it should be optional.                                                                                                                                                                                                         | Make inheritsFrom optional.                                                                                                                                                   |
| node:security.accesscondition#4           | real_defect (0.59)       | attribute_typing (0.77)          | 2.3 | 1.8 | 0.12  | 0.05 | The value attribute of security.accesscondition is string-only, so it cannot represent non-string values such as numbers, booleans or lists.                                                                                                                                                                                | Allow value to hold non-string types (e.g. number, boolean, list) rather than string only.                                                                                    |
| node:security.securitypolicy#3            | real_defect (0.65)       | missing_attribute (0.60)         | 2.2 | 1.3 | 0.10  | 0.09 | The security.securitypolicy 'target' attribute holds only a category enum, so the concrete target identifier is missing.                                                                                                                                                                                                    | Add a target identifier attribute (e.g., targetRef or targetId) that names the concrete target alongside the category.                                                        |
| node:security.delegation#1                | real_defect (0.72)       | enum_gap (0.81)                  | 2.0 | 2.0 | 0.05  | 0.04 | The attribute security.delegation.delegationType mixes orthogonal axes (scope: full/partial; time: temporary; condition: conditional; revocability: revocable) in a single enum, although a delegation can be partial and temporary at once.                                                                                | Split delegationType into a scope attribute (full/partial) plus a separate boolean revocable attribute, with time and condition expressed by other attributes.                |
| node:security.auditconfig#4               | real_defect (0.86)       | attribute_typing (0.86)          | 1.9 | 1.9 | 0.27  | 0.04 | security.auditconfig.sanitizeFields is typed as a string although it holds multiple fields, and is marked required although it is arguably optional.                                                                                                                                                                        | Change sanitizeFields to an array of strings and make it optional.                                                                                                            |
| rel:security.securitymodel#1              | real_defect (0.83)       | relationship_redundant (0.73) ×3 | 1.8 | 1.8 | 0.11  | 0.06 | SecurityModel links to AuthenticationConfig with both aggregates and composes, which is contradictory; only composes should remain since there is one config per application.                                                                                                                                               | Remove the aggregates relationship from SecurityModel to AuthenticationConfig and keep composes.                                                                              |
| node:security.delegation#2                | real_defect (0.56)       | missing_attribute (0.81)         | 1.8 | 1.1 | 0.08  | 0.04 | security.delegation has no attribute naming the delegated scope, so partial delegations cannot record the role or permission list being delegated.                                                                                                                                                                          | Add an attribute (or equivalent) that identifies the delegated scope, such as a role or permission list.                                                                      |
| rel:security.auditconfig#1                | real_defect (0.80)       | relationship_direction (0.41)    | 1.8 | 1.7 | 0.59  | 0.33 | The relationships security.auditconfig depends-on security.retentionpolicy and security.auditconfig governs security.retentionpolicy point in opposite directions relative to security.retentionpolicy governs security.auditconfig, and governs contradicts the scope statement that retention belongs to RetentionPolicy. | Remove the governs relationship from security.auditconfig to security.retentionpolicy.                                                                                        |
| rel:security.countermeasure#1             | real_defect (0.72)       | relationship_direction (0.72)    | 1.8 | 1.7 | 0.45  | 0.07 | The relationship security.countermeasure implements security.securitypolicy points the wrong way: concrete configuration implements countermeasures, not the reverse.                                                                                                                                                       | Remove or reverse the implements relationship so that the policy or configuration implements the countermeasure.                                                              |
| node:security.permission#1                | real_defect (0.78)       | enum_gap (0.75)                  | 1.7 | 1.8 | 0.30  | 0.15 | The security.permission scope enum mixes access level values (read-only, write, admin) with grant mechanism values (delegated, conditional), mixing two different axes.                                                                                                                                                     | Restrict scope to access level values and move delegated/conditional to a separate attribute or to other types such as delegation or conditions.                              |
| rel:security.threat#2                     | real_defect (0.73)       | relationship_direction (0.61)    | 1.7 | 1.8 | 0.25  | 0.06 | The relationship security.threat aggregates security.countermeasure inverts the natural 'countermeasure mitigates threat' direction and is not a whole-part relationship.                                                                                                                                                   | Remove the aggregates relationship and rely on security.countermeasure mitigates security.threat.                                                                             |
